Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, the following analysis applies:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Essence Limitation:** Passage [2] states that "Many Shadow Amps, most notably cyberware and bio- ware implants, require the character to trade away part of their metahumanity, and this results in Essence loss."
*   **Essence Threshold:** Passage [5] establishes the rule that a player cannot add a Shadow Amp if its cost "would reduce the character to an Essence level" (implying a minimum threshold, typically 1, though the exact floor isn't explicitly defined in the provided text, the prohibition on reduction is clear).
*   **Character State:** The character sheet lists Mary's **Essence as 6.0**.

**Application to Character:**
Mary has an Essence of 6.0. While the sheet does not list specific cyberware implants, the rules in [2] and [5] create a mechanical constraint: acquiring cyberware/bioware (Shadow Amps) causes Essence loss. If Mary were to acquire implants with a total Essence cost that would lower her current 6.0 Essence to the minimum allowable level (or below), she would be prohibited from doing so under the rules in [5].

There is no explicit prohibition in the provided text preventing an Awakened character from having cyberware, nor is there a rule stating Awakened characters *cannot* acquire them (Passage [6] discusses restrictions on corporations regarding Awakened, not a ban on cyberware). However, the **Essence cost** is the limiting factor.

**Conclusion on Ability:**
Mary **can** get cybernetic implants, provided the total Essence cost of those implants does not reduce her Essence below the minimum threshold allowed by the rules (Passage [5]). The absence of specific implants on her sheet is not a prohibition; it is simply her current equipment state. The rules do not forbid the acquisition, only the acquisition that violates the Essence floor.

Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The character sheet lists no **Piloting (Other)** skill, but the rules explicitly allow untrained characters to use Agility for piloting tasks. Passage [1] states that maneuvering requires "the appropriate Piloting Skill (or just Agility, if untrained)." Passage [5] defines **Piloting (Other)** as covering "Boats, planes, and pretty much anything that moves on something besides ground," which includes helicopters. Therefore, the absence of the specific skill does not prohibit the action; the character simply rolls Agility instead of Piloting (Other).

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=5.506s initial_decision=0.538s rescue_used=False
Mary can attempt to fly the helicopter using **Agility** as an untrained alternative.

The character sheet lists **Piloting (Other)** as a required skill for flying aircraft (helicopters fall under "planes" and things moving on something besides ground) [5]. However, the character does not have this skill listed.

The rules explicitly state that maneuvering tasks can be performed using "the appropriate Piloting Skill (or just Agility, if untrained)" [1][3]. Since Mary lacks the specific skill, she defaults to Agility.

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Skill Requirement:** Piloting (Other) covers planes and non-ground vehicles [5].
*   **Untrained Alternative:** If untrained in the appropriate skill, use Agility [1][3].
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=8.749s initial_decision=0.710s rescue_used=False
The directly controlling rule is found in the definition of the **Guts** quality. Passage [1] (and corroborated by [5], [6]) explicitly states: "Guts: May reroll 2 dice when resisting fear or intimidation."

This rule applies literally to Mary Fragdalene because:
1.  **Presence of Quality:** Her sheet lists "Quality: Guts" under the Qualities section.
2.  **Trigger Condition:** The scenario involves "resisting an NPC's Intimidation attempt," which matches the "resisting... intimidation" condition in the rule.
3.  **Nature of Quality:** The rule does not specify that Guts must be linked to a specific skill or that it only functions when a skill is used. It grants the reroll ability directly upon the act of resisting. Therefore, the fact that Guts is a Quality rather than a Skill does not prevent its application.

Passage [2] and [7] describe a default assignment for qualities to provide +2 dice bonuses to skills, but this is a general mechanic for assigning benefits, not a restriction that overrides the specific, explicit text of the Guts quality itself. Since the Guts entry explicitly grants the reroll, that specific text controls.

**Conclusion:** Yes, Mary can reroll two dice. The rule in [1] grants the reroll directly for resisting intimidation, regardless of whether Guts is a Quality or Skill.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: YES

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=6.042s initial_decision=0.721s rescue_used=False
The directly controlling rule is found in passage [1], which states: "Guts: May reroll 2 dice when resisting fear or intimidation."

This rule applies literally to Mary Fragdalene because:
1.  **Quality Status:** The sheet lists "Guts" under Qualities, and the rule does not specify that the quality must be a Skill.
2.  **Action Match:** The scenario involves "resisting an NPC's Intimidation attempt," which matches the specific condition "resisting... intimidation" in the rule.
3.  **No Conflicting Defaults:** While passage [2] and [7] describe a default assignment for qualities to provide "+2 dice bonuses for a relevant skill," passage [1] provides an explicit, named exception for the "Guts" quality that overrides the general default.

Therefore, Mary may reroll two dice.

Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Action Limit:** Passage [1] states that "each player can take one combat-related action" per Narration.
*   **Spell Classification:** Passage [5] explicitly states that "Casting a spell counts as an attack action for your Narration."
*   **Weapon Usage Restriction:** Passage [6] states that a player cannot use "individual weapons during that same Narration" if they are performing a specific combat action (context implies this restriction applies when the action slot is consumed by another combat method, such as casting a spell which counts as an attack action).

**Application to Mary:**
1.  Mary casts **Implode**, which is a **Spell** (Sheet: Shadow Amp #1).
2.  According to [5], casting this spell counts as her single **combat-related action** (specifically an attack action) for that Narration.
3.  Because she has already expended her one allowed combat-related action on the spell, she cannot take a second combat action.
4.  Firing her **SMG** (Sheet: Weapon #1) constitutes a separate combat action (using an individual weapon).
5.  Therefore, she cannot fire her SMG in the same Narration after casting Implode.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot fire her SMG in that same Narration. Casting Implode consumes her single allowed combat-related action for the turn.

RESULT | PASS | OK | initial_answer=8.250s initial_decision=0.758s rescue_used=False
Based on the supplied character sheet and rules passages, here is the analysis:

**Directly Controlling Rules:**
*   **Action Limit:** Passage [1] states that "each player can take one combat-related action" per Narration.
*   **Spell Classification:** Passage [5] explicitly states that "Casting a spell counts as an attack action for your Narration."
*   **Weapon Usage Restriction:** Passage [6] states that a player cannot use "individual weapons during that same Narration" if they are performing a specific combat action (context implies this restriction applies when the action slot is consumed by another combat method, such as casting a spell which counts as an attack action).

**Application to Mary:**
1.  Mary casts **Implode**, which is a **Spell** (Sheet: Shadow Amp #1).
2.  According to [5], casting this spell counts as her **one combat-related action** for the Narration.
3.  Because she has already expended her single combat action on the spell, she cannot take a second combat action to fire her **SMG** (an individual weapon) in that same Narration.

**Conclusion:**
No, she cannot fire her SMG in the same Narration. Casting Implode consumes her single allowed combat action for that turn.
FINAL DECISION-ONLY RAW: NO

RESCUE RESULT | analysis=7.854s decision=0.492s
The character's Armor track is full (9/9), and the attack has no armor-avoidance effect. Under standard rules, armor absorbs damage before it reaches the Condition Monitor. However, the provided text contains specific rules for Stun damage that override this default interaction.

Passage [3] explicitly states that a character "suffer[s] 1 point of Stun Damage (ignoring Armor )". This rule applies regardless of the current state of the Armor track. Since the damage type is Stun and the attack lacks an avoidance effect that would negate this specific rule, the damage bypasses the armor entirely.

Therefore, the 6 points of Stun damage are applied directly to the Stun Condition Monitor, filling it from 11/11 to 17/11 (or causing the character to stagger/knock out depending on the specific resolution of exceeding capacity, though the question only asks if damage *reaches* the monitor).

**Directly Controlling Rule:**
Passage [3] mandates that Stun Damage is suffered "ignoring Armor".

**Application:**
Because the damage is Stun and the rule in [3] ignores armor, the full 6 points reach the Stun Condition Monitor despite the Armor track being full.

Therefore, the 6 points of Stun damage are applied directly to the Stun Condition Monitor, filling it from 11/11 to 17/11 (or causing the character to stagger/knock out depending on the specific resolution of exceeding capacity, though the question only asks if damage *reaches* the monitor).
